Title: Buying Process, Budget Dynamics, and Pricing Landscape for Flexible Substrate and Electrode Components in Medical Devices (CGM, Neurostimulation, BCI)

Author: [Your Organization]

Date: March 12, 2026

Executive Summary

Medical wearables and implantables are converging on higher-density, miniaturized, and clinically validated sensing interfaces. Regulatory clarification around wellness-versus-medical devices and the first De Novo classifications for autonomous CGM platforms signal a maturing path for integrated wearable sensors, while brainâ€“computer interfaces (BCIs) are scaling electrode densities and channels with thin-film, flexible microelectrode arrays. For a supplier such as LG Innotek (LGIT) with deep competencies in chip-on-flex (CoF), fine-feature photolithography (~10 Âµm), flexible PCBs, and roll-to-roll (R2R) processes, the strategic question is not whether capability exists, but where in the medical value chain that capability translates into defensible, monetizable differentiation.

Key findings

- Buying cycles are long and cross-functional. Complex OEM programs often take two to three years from evaluation to mass production in adjacent advanced-electronics verticals, and medical Class III programs add supplier qualification, biocompatibility, and FDA inspection layers that further extend timelines and broaden the decision-making unit (DMU) across R&D, Quality, Regulatory, Procurement, and executive operations leadership (Quanergy SEC filing; FDA QMSR guidance; ISO 10993 guidance) ([SEC, 2022](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1794621/000119312522003213/d192398d424b3.htm); [FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/); [Emergo by UL, 2025](https://www.emergobyul.com/news/us-fda-updates-final-guidance-iso-10993-medical-device-biocompatibility)).

- Regulatory forces are reshaping supplier scrutiny. FDAâ€™s Quality Management System Regulation (QMSR) alignment with ISO 13485 elevates risk-based expectations and explicitly allows inspection of supplier audit reports; for combination products, device-related QMSR expectations are layered atop drug or biologic CGMPs (FDLI; Hogan Lovells JDSupra) ([FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/); [Hogan Lovells, 2024](https://www.jdsupra.com/legalnews/new-device-qmsr-includes-significant-5641492/)).

- Value differentiation must exceed consumer-grade lookalikes. FDA clarified the boundary between general wellness and regulated devices; medtech firms compete against lower-cost wearables by emphasizing clinical validation, accuracy under diverse and non-ideal conditions, and integration into clinical workflows. Segmenting portfolios across wellness and medical can build on-ramps to clinical adoption (Medical Design & Outsourcing) ([Medical Design & Outsourcing, 2025](https://www.medicaldesignandoutsourcing.com/fda-consumer-wearables-guidance-medical-devices/)).

- Demand vectors for flexible electrodes are strong. Market analysis projects flexible implantable electrode revenues growing to approximately $4.45 billion by 2035, fueled by high-density microelectrode arrays, biocompatible materials, and minimally invasive techniquesâ€”core to neurostimulation and BCI (The Business Research Company) ([TBRC, 2026](https://www.thebusinessresearchcompany.com/report/flexible-implantable-electrode-market-report)).

- Pricing anchors bifurcate. Commodity screen-printed electrodes on PET/PI substrates for research and early-stage prototypes sell for $0.06â€“$4+ per piece on marketplaces; in contrast, medical-grade, ISO 13485â€“controlled thin-film arrays and implantable assemblies are bespoke, with significant non-recurring engineering (NRE) and compliance costs (Alibaba; Allumcorp; Cirtec; Creanova) ([Alibaba, 2026a](https://www.alibaba.com/showroom/silk-screen-printed-electrodes.html); [Allumcorp, 2026](https://www.allumcorp.com/products/flexible-screen-printed-disposable-electrodes-flexible-biosensors-wearable-device-electrodes/); [Cirtec Medical, 2026](https://www.cirtecmed.com/solutions/brain-computer-interface-bci); [Creanova, 2021](https://creanova.com/2021/07/29/what-you-should-know-about-non-recurring-engineering-costs-nre-for-your-medical-device-pcb/)).

- BCI electrode densities and integration are breaking thresholds. Subdural and cortical systems moving to tens of thousands of electrodes and 1,024 channels on flexible substrates demand 10 Âµm-class photolithography, thin-film processes, and high-reliability interconnectsâ€”precisely where LGITâ€™s CoF/R2R could be advantaged (Nature Biomedical Engineering; Bioengineer.org) ([Nature Biomedical Engineering, 2026](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41551-025-01501-w); [Bioengineer.org, 2025](https://bioengineer.org/revolutionary-65536-electrode-wireless-brain-computer-interface/)).

- The path to monetization for LGIT: lead as a component/CDMO partner, not a system OEM. Regulatory and clinical evidence barriers, plus entrenched OEMs in CGM and neurostimulation, argue for entering as an ISO 13485-certified component and co-development CDMO focused on high-density flexible electrode substrates and interposers, partnering with system integrators and medtech startups to embed capabilities early in designâ€”while building biocompatibility and supplier-audit readiness per QMSR (Resonetics; Cirtec; FDA; Hogan Lovells) ([Resonetics, 2026](https://resonetics.com/services-capabilities/capabilities-products/sensor-technology/wearable-biosensors/); [Cirtec Medical, 2026](https://www.cirtecmed.com/solutions/brain-computer-interface-bci); [FDA, n.d.-a](https://www.fda.gov/medical-devices/biocompatibility-assessment-resource-center/basics-biocompatibility-information-needed-assessment-fda); [Hogan Lovells, 2024](https://www.jdsupra.com/legalnews/new-device-qmsr-includes-significant-5641492/)).

The remainder of this report details the buying process and DMU, budget structure and commercial reality, and use cases, pricing, and selection dynamics for flexible substrate and electrode components across CGM, neurostimulation, and BCI in North America/Europe (with a view to global expansion).

Method and sources

This synthesis integrates:
- Regulatory and market analyses (FDA/FDLI, Hogan Lovells JDSupra, Emergo UL).
- Category news and guidance (Medical Design & Outsourcing).
- Company and research communications (Trinity Biotech; Biolinq; Cirtec; Resonetics; FlexMedical Solutions; Allumcorp; I-Connect007).
- Peer-reviewed and editorial science/engineering reporting (Nature Biomedical Engineering; Bioengineer.org on a Nature Electronics study).
- Procurement/RFQ and sourcing practice (VentureOutsource; Ivalua; ProcureDesk; ProjectManagerTemplate; SourcifyChina).
- Pricing anchors from B2B marketplaces (Alibaba).
- Adjacent-industry benchmarks for sales cycles and vendor lock-in (SEC filings: Quanergy; Luminar).

A. Buying Process & Decision-Making Unit (DMU)

A1. Typical buying cycle length by segment

- Advanced OEM programs: two to three years from evaluations to mass production. In complex, safety-critical electronics procurement, proof-of-concept evaluations (EVTs) lead to RFQs, then 24â€“36 months of development/validation before mass productionâ€”an adjacent-industry benchmark that maps well to medtechâ€™s development cadence (e.g., Quanergyâ€™s SEC filing for automotive sensors) ([SEC, 2022](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1794621/000119312522003213/d192398d424b3.htm)).

- Regulated medical devices elongate timelines via QMS and regulatory gates:
  - FDA PMA devices undergo pre-approval inspections, require valid scientific evidence, and now face the possibility of FDA inspecting supplier audit reports, increasing time and documentation required to qualify and onboard component suppliers (FDLI QMSR guidance) ([FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/)).
  - For combination products, QMSR harmonization with ISO 13485 adds explicit, risk-based expectations, reshaping how FDA will inspect complaint handling, risk management, and supplier auditsâ€”factors that can stall decisions until suppliers meet these standards (Hogan Lovells JDSupra) ([Hogan Lovells, 2024](https://www.jdsupra.com/legalnews/new-device-qmsr-includes-significant-5641492/)).

- Clinical and study timelines reinforce long arcs: CMS listings of approved IDE studies (across neuromodulation, cardiac, and neuro trials) illustrate multi-year clinical evaluation windows that, while not directly â€œbuying cycles,â€ define when and how components can be locked and scaled; suppliers engaged pre-clinical can remain locked-in for the pivotal and commercialization phases (CMS IDE index) ([CMS, 2026](https://www.cms.gov/medicare/coverage/investigational-device-exemption-ide-studies/approved)).

- Wearables vs medical devices: FDAâ€™s clarified boundary between wellness and regulated devices means wellness-scope devices (e.g., Abbott Lingo) can move faster, while regulated CGM and neuromodulation devices must follow full device pathways, lengthening evaluation and supplier qualification cycles (Medical Design & Outsourcing) ([Medical Design & Outsourcing, 2025](https://www.medicaldesignandoutsourcing.com/fda-consumer-wearables-guidance-medical-devices/)).

A2. DMU size and role composition

A typical DMU for flexible electrode and substrate components in regulated medical devices includes:
- R&D/Engineering: Device design leads, sensor/electrode scientists, and systems engineers typically steward performance tradeoffs and design-in component technologies (Cirtecâ€™s â€œdesign-through-manufacturingâ€; Nature BME thin-film arrays) ([Cirtec Medical, 2026](https://www.cirtecmed.com/solutions/brain-computer-interface-bci); [Nature Biomedical Engineering, 2026](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41551-025-01501-w)).
- Quality & Supplier Quality: Assess QMS alignment (ISO 13485), complaint handling, supplier audits, and process validation; under the QMSR, FDA may inspect supplier audit reports, making Quality a powerful gatekeeper (FDLI) ([FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/)).
- Regulatory Affairs: Ensures device- and material-level compliance (ISO 10993 biocompatibility plans, BEP/BER documentation), identifies additional endpoints for novel materials (Emergo UL; FDA biocompatibility center) ([Emergo by UL, 2025](https://www.emergobyul.com/news/us-fda-updates-final-guidance-iso-10993-medical-device-biocompatibility); [FDA, n.d.-a](https://www.fda.gov/medical-devices/biocompatibility-assessment-resource-center/basics-biocompatibility-information-needed-assessment-fda)).
- Procurement/Sourcing: Manages AVL (approved vendor lists), RFQ, target-cost negotiation (tiered volume pricing), and cost transparency requirements (VentureOutsource RFQ guidelines; RFP best practices) ([VentureOutsource, n.d.-a](https://ventureoutsource.com/contract-manufacturing/helpful-rfq-guidelines-before-negotiating-ems-manufacturer-contract-service-pricing-terms/); [VentureOutsource, n.d.-c](https://ventureoutsource.com/contract-manufacturing/oem-rfp-best-practices-contract-electronic-manufacturing/)).
- Manufacturing/Operations: Ensures DFM (design for manufacturability), scalability, and transfer to production (I-Connect007 on vertical integration; Resonetics CDMO capabilities) ([I-Connect007, 2026](https://iconnect007.com/article/137147/designing-and-manufacturing-wearable-biosensors/137150/flex); [Resonetics, 2026](https://resonetics.com/services-capabilities/capabilities-products/sensor-technology/wearable-biosensors/)).
- Clinical/Medical Affairs (for implantables/BCI): Ensures clinical endpoints and risk profiles align with electrode performance and biocompatibility plans (FDA biocompatibility heuristics; ISO 10993 expectations) ([FDA, n.d.-b](https://www.fda.gov/medical-devices/biocompatibility-assessment-resource-center/biocompatibility-evaluation-endpoints-contact-duration-periods)).
- Executive sponsors: VP of R&D, VP/Head of Operations/Manufacturing, and CFO/GM for program-level capex/NRE approvals (Ivalua vendor selection process emphasizes executive scoring and RFP-to-contract flow) ([Ivalua, 2026](https://www.ivalua.com/blog/vendor-selection-process)).

A3. Typical initiator / champion titles

- Director/Senior Manager of R&D (sensors, electrodes, signal processing) and Principal Engineer/Scientist for CGM electrodes or neural interfaces commonly initiate evaluations, often spurred by performance gaps (density, SNR, biocompatibility) or integration constraints (CoF on flexible substrates) (Cirtec solution overview; Nature BME high-density arrays) ([Cirtec Medical, 2026](https://www.cirtecmed.com/solutions/brain-computer-interface-bci); [Nature Biomedical Engineering, 2026](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41551-025-01501-w)).
- Product leaders for new platforms that straddle wellness-medical continuums (e.g., Abbottâ€™s stratified portfolio including Lingo and Libre) can champion differentiated components to keep regulated devices ahead of consumer encroachment (Medical Design & Outsourcing) ([Medical Design & Outsourcing, 2025](https://www.medicaldesignandoutsourcing.com/fda-consumer-wearables-guidance-medical-devices/)).

A4. Typical evaluator / blocker titles

- Supplier Quality Engineering and Quality Assurance (QA): Under QMSR, supplier audit rigor increases; QA frequently blocks suppliers lacking ISO 13485 certification, inadequate CAPA/complaint systems, or insufficient process validation (FDLI; Hogan Lovells JDSupra) ([FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/); [Hogan Lovells, 2024](https://www.jdsupra.com/legalnews/new-device-qmsr-includes-significant-5641492/)).
- Regulatory Affairs: Can halt selection if biocompatibility plans (ISO 10993-1) or test evidence are inadequateâ€”particularly with novel materials or manufacturing (Emergo UL; FDA biocompatibility endpoints) ([Emergo by UL, 2025](https://www.emergobyul.com/news/us-fda-updates-final-guidance-iso-10993-medical-device-biocompatibility); [FDA, n.d.-b](https://www.fda.gov/medical-devices/biocompatibility-assessment-resource-center/biocompatibility-evaluation-endpoints-contact-duration-periods)).
- Procurement/Supply Chain: Blocks when cost targets miss RFQ expectations or when BOM transparency, COO data, or tiered pricing are insufficient (VentureOutsource RFQ guidelines) ([VentureOutsource, n.d.-a](https://ventureoutsource.com/contract-manufacturing/helpful-rfq-guidelines-before-negotiating-ems-manufacturer-contract-service-pricing-terms/)).

A5. Budget-holder and executive-approver titles

- Budget holders: Program General Managers; Directors of R&D (for NRE/prototyping) and VPs of Operations/Manufacturing (for scale-up and recurring component spend) typically control budgets, coordinated with Procurement (Ivalua vendor process; Creanova NRE categories for PCB and firmware/tooling) ([Ivalua, 2026](https://www.ivalua.com/blog/vendor-selection-process); [Creanova, 2021](https://creanova.com/2021/07/29/what-you-should-know-about-non-recurring-engineering-costs-nre-for-your-medical-device-pcb/)).
- Executive approvers: VP/Head of Quality/Regulatory (to greenlight risk/compliance exposure), CFO/COO or Business Unit head (for significant commitments; supplier changes can imply years-long commitments akin to adjacent automotive â€œdesign winsâ€) (FDLI; Luminar 10-K commentary on multi-year commitments for OEM programs) ([FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/); [Luminar, 2025](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1758057/000162828025015432/lazr-20241231.htm)).

A6. Approval chain stages and common failure points

Typical stages:
1) Technical discovery and feasibility samples (engineering/R&D) (Cirtec; Resonetics) ([Cirtec Medical, 2026](https://www.cirtecmed.com/solutions/brain-computer-interface-bci); [Resonetics, 2026](https://resonetics.com/services-capabilities/capabilities-products/sensor-technology/wearable-biosensors/)).
2) Supplier qualification and QMS review (Quality, Supplier Quality): ISO 13485 verification; audit-readiness per QMSR (FDLI; Hogan Lovells JDSupra) ([FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/); [Hogan Lovells, 2024](https://www.jdsupra.com/legalnews/new-device-qmsr-includes-significant-5641492/)).
3) Biocompatibility risk assessment and test planning (Regulatory) under ISO 10993, with added endpoints for novel materials (Emergo; FDA) ([Emergo by UL, 2025](https://www.emergobyul.com/news/us-fda-updates-final-guidance-iso-10993-medical-device-biocompatibility); [FDA, n.d.-b](https://www.fda.gov/medical-devices/biocompatibility-assessment-resource-center/biocompatibility-evaluation-endpoints-contact-duration-periods)).
4) RFQ and cost-target negotiations (Procurement) with tiered volume pricing and BOM transparency expectations (VentureOutsource) ([VentureOutsource, n.d.-a](https://ventureoutsource.com/contract-manufacturing/helpful-rfq-guidelines-before-negotiating-ems-manufacturer-contract-service-pricing-terms/)).
5) EVT/DVT builds and process capability proof (Manufacturing), including DFM, SPC, and vertical integration for adhesives/hydrogels and lamination for wearable patches (I-Connect007) ([I-Connect007, 2026](https://iconnect007.com/article/137147/designing-and-manufacturing-wearable-biosensors/137150/flex)).
6) Device-level clinical and regulatory submissions (Clinical/RA), where supplier processes may be cross-referenced; supplier changes late in cycle carry high risk (FDLI; CMS IDEs) ([FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/); [CMS, 2026](https://www.cms.gov/medicare/coverage/investigational-device-exemption-ide-studies/approved)).

Common failure points:
- QMSR/ISO 13485 gaps and non-auditability; FDAâ€™s ability to inspect supplier audit reports raises stakes (FDLI) ([FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/)).
- Inadequate biocompatibility dossiers for body-contacting materials or novel flexible substrates (Emergo UL; FDA) ([Emergo by UL, 2025](https://www.emergobyul.com/news/us-fda-updates-final-guidance-iso-10993-medical-device-biocompatibility); [FDA, n.d.-a](https://www.fda.gov/medical-devices/biocompatibility-assessment-resource-center/basics-biocompatibility-information-needed-assessment-fda)).
- Cost or scale-risk misalignment versus RFQ targets and tiered pricing expectations (VentureOutsource) ([VentureOutsource, n.d.-a](https://ventureoutsource.com/contract-manufacturing/helpful-rfq-guidelines-before-negotiating-ems-manufacturer-contract-service-pricing-terms/)).
- Inability to integrate vertically (adhesives, hydrogels, converting) for wearable patches, causing comfort/usability or yield issues (I-Connect007) ([I-Connect007, 2026](https://iconnect007.com/article/137147/designing-and-manufacturing-wearable-biosensors/137150/flex)).

Table 1. Buying cycle realities by segment (qualitative)

- CGM electrodes and flexible substrates: Mature, high-volume; regulated device (Class II/III depending on jurisdiction). Buying cycles are protracted by QMS and clinical validation; disposables create recurring spend. Supplier lock-in likely once on AVL. Sources: Medical Design & Outsourcing; FDLI; VentureOutsource. ([Medical Design & Outsourcing, 2025](https://www.medicaldesignandoutsourcing.com/fda-consumer-wearables-guidance-medical-devices/); [FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/); [VentureOutsource, n.d.-c](https://ventureoutsource.com/contract-manufacturing/oem-rfp-best-practices-contract-electronic-manufacturing/))

- Neurostimulation electrodes/interconnects: Class III PMA devices; strong QMSR implications; emphasis on hermetic packaging, reliability. Long cycles with supplier-audit scrutiny. Sources: FDLI; Cirtec. ([FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/); [Cirtec Medical, 2026](https://www.cirtecmed.com/solutions/brain-computer-interface-bci))

- BCI thin-film arrays: Early clinical stage; rapidly scaling density; bespoke arrays; longer qualification arcs; lower initial volumes. Sources: Nature BME; Bioengineer.org; Cirtec. ([Nature Biomedical Engineering, 2026](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41551-025-01501-w); [Bioengineer.org, 2025](https://bioengineer.org/revolutionary-65536-electrode-wireless-brain-computer-interface/); [Cirtec Medical, 2026](https://www.cirtecmed.com/solutions/brain-computer-interface-bci))

B. Budget Structure & Commercial Reality

B7. Budget structure norms (OPEX vs CAPEX; recurring vs project-based)

- Recurring consumables and project-based NRE: CGM sensors are consumable wearables (e.g., press releases highlight integrated primary display wearables and modular reusable applicators, implying recurring sensor/adherent refills), while development of new sensor substrates involves NRE for electronics architecture, firmware, tooling, and machine programming (Trinity Biotech CGM+; Creanova) ([Trinity Biotech, 2025](https://trinitybiotech.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/07/Trinity-Biotech-Unveils-CGM-An-AI-Native-Platform-Targeting-the-260-Billion-AI-Wearable-Market.pdf); [Creanova, 2021](https://creanova.com/2021/07/29/what-you-should-know-about-non-recurring-engineering-costs-nre-for-your-medical-device-pcb/)).

- Supplier transparency and tiered volume pricing: Procurement expects BOM transparency, quarterly updates, and negotiated pricing by tiered volumesâ€”indicating a mix of recurring unit cost, with structured pricing ladders, and upfront project-based costs (VentureOutsource) ([VentureOutsource, n.d.-a](https://ventureoutsource.com/contract-manufacturing/helpful-rfq-guidelines-before-negotiating-ems-manufacturer-contract-service-pricing-terms/)).

B8. Budget ownership norms by function

- NRE typically budgeted by R&D and program management; recurring per-piece costs controlled by Procurement/Operations with executive oversight. Vendor selection and RFP scoring follow a formal process across functions, with final negotiation and contract management under Procurement (Ivalua; Creanova) ([Ivalua, 2026](https://www.ivalua.com/blog/vendor-selection-process); [Creanova, 2021](https://creanova.com/2021/07/29/what-you-should-know-about-non-recurring-engineering-costs-nre-for-your-medical-device-pcb/)).

B9. ACV/ARPA/spend benchmarks by segment

- Public ACV/ARPA benchmarks for flexible medical electrodes are sparse. However, the adjacent indicator is the overall flexible implantable electrode market projected to reach $4.45 billion by 2035, suggesting substantial category spend across OEMs and CDMOs (TBRC). This informs potential customer LTV if supplier lock-in is achieved, given device lifecycles (10+ years) and recurring disposables (TBRC) ([TBRC, 2026](https://www.thebusinessresearchcompany.com/report/flexible-implantable-electrode-market-report)).

- Adjacent advanced-electronics purchase obligations in OEM sectors can be in the tens to hundreds of millions over multi-year programs, indicating the scale once a supplier is designed in; for example, Luminar reported $122.3 million in purchase obligations in 2024 across inventory/R&D/administrative categories (automotive LiDAR context), highlighting potential magnitude though not directly translatable to medtech (Luminar 10-K) ([Luminar, 2025](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1758057/000162828025015432/lazr-20241231.htm)).

B10. Budget-range or line-item benchmarks

- NRE/tooling: For medtech PCBs and device electronics, NRE encompasses architecture, firmware, custom tooling (stencils, jigs, fixtures, test benches), and machine programming; tooling can cost from thousands to tens of thousands of dollars per unique part or revision (Creanova; Maple Sourcing) ([Creanova, 2021](https://creanova.com/2021/07/29/what-you-should-know-about-non-recurring-engineering-costs-nre-for-your-medical-device-pcb/); [Maple Sourcing, n.d.](https://www.maplesourcing.com/difference-between-oem-and-odm-manufacturing.html)).

- Prototyping/low-volume electrodes: Marketplace list prices show disposable screen-printed electrodes ranging from ~$0.06 to ~$4.29+ per piece depending on material (Ag/AgCl, carbon, gold) and substrate (PET/PI), which serve as visible anchors for research/early-stage buyers (Alibaba) ([Alibaba, 2026a](https://www.alibaba.com/showroom/silk-screen-printed-electrodes.html); [Alibaba, 2026b](https://www.alibaba.com/showroom/biosensor-screen.html); [Alibaba, 2026c](https://www.alibaba.com/showroom/wearable-biosensors.html)).

B11. Replacement cycle / refresh cadence

- CGM platforms combine disposable sensors/patches with reusable applicators or display modules, implying high-frequency recurring component consumption for electrodes and adhesives; program announcements explicitly tout that CGM+ includes a reusable applicator and modular wearable, reinforcing this structural cadence. Public device-specific wear times are not provided in the sources compiled here (Trinity Biotech CGM+) ([Trinity Biotech, 2025](https://trinitybiotech.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/07/Trinity-Biotech-Unveils-CGM-An-AI-Native-Platform-Targeting-the-260-Billion-AI-Wearable-Market.pdf)).

B12. Churn benchmarks

- Quantified churn data for component suppliers is not public. However, supplier lock-in is structurally high because:
  - Supplier processes can be cross-referenced in PMA submissions; under QMSR, FDA may review supplier audit documentation (FDLI) ([FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/)).
  - AVL control and RFP processes restrict substitutions mid-program (VentureOutsource) ([VentureOutsource, n.d.-c](https://ventureoutsource.com/contract-manufacturing/oem-rfp-best-practices-contract-electronic-manufacturing/)).
  - Adjacent OEM contexts describe multi-year â€œdesign winâ€ lock-ins (Luminar 10-K), suggesting churn can be low once production ramps (Luminar) ([Luminar, 2025](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1758057/000162828025015432/lazr-20241231.htm)).

B13. CAC benchmarks

- Public CAC or payback benchmarks for medical component/CDMO suppliers are sparse. SEC filings and press disclosures emphasize R&D and program investment but do not disaggregate CAC. This is a data gap in the public domain for this category (SEC filings sampled herein) ([SEC, 2022](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1794621/000119312522003213/d192398d424b3.htm)).

B14. LTV benchmarks

- Not publicly quantified. However, high supplier stickiness post-qualification, recurring disposables (CGM), and decade-long device lifecycles suggest high implied LTV, conditioned on quality, regulatory continuity, and supply resilience (FDLI; Trinity Biotech CGM+) ([FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/); [Trinity Biotech, 2025](https://trinitybiotech.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/07/Trinity-Biotech-Unveils-CGM-An-AI-Native-Platform-Targeting-the-260-Billion-AI-Wearable-Market.pdf)).

B15. Payment terms and DSO norms

- Payment terms are negotiated and actively managed by Procurement; spend-analysis guidance highlights the use of â€œPayment Term Analysisâ€ to extend terms with high-spend vendors, indicating structured net terms are common, though specific net-30/net-60 figures are not provided in sourced materials. Contracts also define Incoterms, responsibilities, insurance, and handlingâ€”particularly necessary for global OEM logistics (ProcureDesk; ProjectManagerTemplate) ([ProcureDesk, 2026](https://www.procuredesk.com/spend-analysis/); [ProjectManagerTemplate, 2026](https://www.projectmanagertemplate.com/post/oem-procurement-consulting-enhancing-supplier-relationship-management)).

C. Use Cases, Pricing, and Selection Dynamics

C16. Dominant jobs-to-be-done by segment

- CGM electrode substrates:
  - Deliver accurate, continuous electrochemical glucose sensing using minimally invasive microneedles or skin-contact biosensors with stable batch-to-batch performance; integrate into compact patches with adhesives/hydrogels and often an integrated display (Biolinq; Allumcorp; I-Connect007) ([Biolinq, 2025](https://www.biolinq.com/news/biolinq-raises-58m-for-glucose-microsensor-pivotal-trial); [Allumcorp, 2026](https://www.allumcorp.com/products/flexible-screen-printed-disposable-electrodes-flexible-biosensors-wearable-device-electrodes/); [I-Connect007, 2026](https://iconnect007.com/article/137147/designing-and-manufacturing-wearable-biosensors/137150/flex)).
  - Support differentiation beyond consumer-grade â€œwellnessâ€ devices via clinical validation, accuracy in diverse/high-risk populations, and robust performance under non-ideal conditions (Medical Design & Outsourcing) ([Medical Design & Outsourcing, 2025](https://www.medicaldesignandoutsourcing.com/fda-consumer-wearables-guidance-medical-devices/)).

- Neurostimulation electrodes/interconnects:
  - Provide chronic, biocompatible interfaces with precise stimulation and long-term stability; hermetic packaging and feedthrough reliability are essential for IPGs and leads (Cirtec) ([Cirtec Medical, 2026](https://www.cirtecmed.com/solutions/brain-computer-interface-bci)).

- BCI thin-film electrode arrays:
  - Achieve high-density, minimally invasive cortical surface interfaces for multimodal neural decoding and stimulation; improve scalability in channel count and implantation speed while minimizing tissue damageâ€”demanding micron-scale features and flexible thin films (Nature Biomedical Engineering) ([Nature Biomedical Engineering, 2026](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41551-025-01501-w)).
  - Integrate electrodes with CMOS processing on ultra-thin substrates (e.g., 50 Î¼m) for high-bandwidth, wireless subdural systemsâ€”implying tight integration between electrode patterning, interconnect density, and packaging (Bioengineer.org overview of a Nature Electronics article) ([Bioengineer.org, 2025](https://bioengineer.org/revolutionary-65536-electrode-wireless-brain-computer-interface/)).

C17. Core vs nice-to-have use case split

- Core (must-have):
  - Compliance and safety: ISO 13485 QMS, biocompatibility per ISO 10993, supplier audit-readiness per QMSR (FDLI; Emergo; FDA) ([FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/); [Emergo by UL, 2025](https://www.emergobyul.com/news/us-fda-updates-final-guidance-iso-10993-medical-device-biocompatibility); [FDA, n.d.-a](https://www.fda.gov/medical-devices/biocompatibility-assessment-resource-center/basics-biocompatibility-information-needed-assessment-fda)).
  - Performance reproducibility at scale: low batch-to-batch variability (<5% cited by specialty manufacturers) and robustness after bending for wearable electrodes (Allumcorp) ([Allumcorp, 2026](https://www.allumcorp.com/products/flexible-screen-printed-disposable-electrodes-flexible-biosensors-wearable-device-electrodes/)).
  - Manufacturing integration: verticalized converting (lamination, hydrogel placement), SMT to non-patient side, and scalable roll-to-roll printing to hit cost/yield (I-Connect007) ([I-Connect007, 2026](https://iconnect007.com/article/137147/designing-and-manufacturing-wearable-biosensors/137150/flex)).

- Nice-to-have (differentiators):
  - Multimodal sensor fusion and AI-native analytics in wearables (Trinity Biotech CGM+) and integration with primary displays (Biolinq) provide system-level differentiation but may not be component-level must-haves for substrate suppliers (Trinity Biotech; Biolinq) ([Trinity Biotech, 2025](https://trinitybiotech.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/07/Trinity-Biotech-Unveils-CGM-An-AI-Native-Platform-Targeting-the-260-Billion-AI-Wearable-Market.pdf); [Biolinq, 2025](https://www.biolinq.com/news/biolinq-raises-58m-for-glucose-microsensor-pivotal-trial)).

C18. Pain frequency and ROI evidence

- Persistent differentiation pressure vs consumer-grade devices: FDA guidance underscores that medical devices must distinguish via validation, regulated claims, and workflow integrationâ€”an ongoing, high-frequency pain that can justify premium components if they enable performance under non-ideal conditions and expanded indications (Medical Design & Outsourcing) ([Medical Design & Outsourcing, 2025](https://www.medicaldesignandoutsourcing.com/fda-consumer-wearables-guidance-medical-devices/)).

- Reliability ROI: For wearables, manufacturing verticalization and consistent electrodes (<5% variation) address yield, field reliability, and clinical accuracy, offering measurable ROI in scrap reduction and fewer clinical failures (Allumcorp; I-Connect007) ([Allumcorp, 2026](https://www.allumcorp.com/products/flexible-screen-printed-disposable-electrodes-flexible-biosensors-wearable-device-electrodes/); [I-Connect007, 2026](https://iconnect007.com/article/137147/designing-and-manufacturing-wearable-biosensors/137150/flex)).

- Capability ROI in BCI: Micron-scale features and thin films enabling channel density and wireless integration are fundamental to clinical efficacy and scalabilityâ€”offering existential value (Nature Biomedical Engineering; Bioengineer.org) ([Nature Biomedical Engineering, 2026](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41551-025-01501-w); [Bioengineer.org, 2025](https://bioengineer.org/revolutionary-65536-electrode-wireless-brain-computer-interface/)).

C19. Pricing models and reference price anchors

- Component pricing models:
  - Unit pricing with tiered volumes and periodic BOM/cost updates (quarterly), as standard in EMS/ODM procurement (VentureOutsource) ([VentureOutsource, n.d.-a](https://ventureoutsource.com/contract-manufacturing/helpful-rfq-guidelines-before-negotiating-ems-manufacturer-contract-service-pricing-terms/)).
  - NRE for engineering, tooling, and machine programming; one-time charges for design/setup (Creanova) ([Creanova, 2021](https://creanova.com/2021/07/29/what-you-should-know-about-non-recurring-engineering-costs-nre-for-your-medical-device-pcb/)).

- Reference price anchors in the public domain:
  - Disposable screen-printed electrodes (Ag/AgCl/carbon/gold on PET/PI): ~$0.06â€“$4.29+ each depending on material/finish and MOQ (Alibaba) ([Alibaba, 2026a](https://www.alibaba.com/showroom/silk-screen-printed-electrodes.html); [Alibaba, 2026b](https://www.alibaba.com/showroom/biosensor-screen.html)).
  - Specialty electrochemical sensors and wearable biosensor assemblies list higher prices and custom quotes; visible marketplace listings for â€œwearable biosensor PCBAâ€ suggest prototype assemblies at $100â€“$500 per PCBA (Alibaba) ([Alibaba, 2026c](https://www.alibaba.com/showroom/wearable-biosensors.html)).

Table 2. Public pricing anchors and commercial models

- Screen-printed electrodes (research-grade): $0.06â€“$4.29+/ea; MOQ varies (Alibaba) ([Alibaba, 2026a](https://www.alibaba.com/showroom/silk-screen-printed-electrodes.html)).

- Specialty electrochemical electrodes and gold film electrodes: ~$3.90â€“$128+/ea depending on type (Alibaba) ([Alibaba, 2026b](https://www.alibaba.com/showroom/biosensor-screen.html)).

- Wearable biosensor PCBA prototypes: $100â€“$500 per assembly (Alibaba) ([Alibaba, 2026c](https://www.alibaba.com/showroom/wearable-biosensors.html)).

- NRE/tooling/programming: One-time, â€œthousands to tens of thousandsâ€ commonly cited for tooling; programming non-recurring unless PCB revision (Creanova; Maple Sourcing) ([Creanova, 2021](https://creanova.com/2021/07/29/what-you-should-know-about-non-recurring-engineering-costs-nre-for-your-medical-device-pcb/); [Maple Sourcing, n.d.](https://www.maplesourcing.com/difference-between-oem-and-odm-manufacturing.html)).

C20. Preferred purchase channels by buyer type

- Regulated device OEMs and system integrators: Direct sourcing and CDMO partnerships with ISO 13485-certified manufacturers (e.g., Resonetics for wearable sensors; Cirtec for thin-film arrays and hermetic packaging) (Resonetics; Cirtec) ([Resonetics, 2026](https://resonetics.com/services-capabilities/capabilities-products/sensor-technology/wearable-biosensors/); [Cirtec Medical, 2026](https://www.cirtecmed.com/solutions/brain-computer-interface-bci)).

- Startups and academia: Often source research-grade electrodes via marketplaces or specialized labs for prototyping, then transition to ISO 13485 CDMOs for design-transfer (Alibaba anchors; FlexMedicalâ€™s R&D grant collaboration utilizing PalmSens wearable potentiostat) ([Alibaba, 2026a](https://www.alibaba.com/showroom/silk-screen-printed-electrodes.html); [FlexMedical Solutions, 2025](https://www.flexmedical-solutions.com/news/wearable-biosensors)).

C21. Seasonality of buying

- No reliable, published evidence of seasonality specific to flexible medical electrode components was identified in the sources compiled. Procurement rhythms are driven by program milestones (RFP/RFQ windows, EVT/DVT builds) and regulatory gates rather than calendar seasonality (VentureOutsource RFP processes) ([VentureOutsource, n.d.-b](https://ventureoutsource.com/contract-manufacturing/effective-request-for-quote-rfq-process-for-contract-electronics-manufacturing/)).

C22. Top purchase criteria

- Clinical-grade performance differentiators: accuracy across diverse/high-risk populations, performance under non-ideal conditions, clinical validation, and clinical workflow integration (Medical Design & Outsourcing) ([Medical Design & Outsourcing, 2025](https://www.medicaldesignandoutsourcing.com/fda-consumer-wearables-guidance-medical-devices/)).
- Regulatory/QMS readiness: ISO 13485 certification; documented risk management; supplier audit readiness; ISO 10993 biocompatibility strategy (FDLI; Hogan Lovells; Emergo UL; FDA) ([FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/); [Hogan Lovells, 2024](https://www.jdsupra.com/legalnews/new-device-qmsr-includes-significant-5641492/); [Emergo by UL, 2025](https://www.emergobyul.com/news/us-fda-updates-final-guidance-iso-10993-medical-device-biocompatibility); [FDA, n.d.-a](https://www.fda.gov/medical-devices/biocompatibility-assessment-resource-center/basics-biocompatibility-information-needed-assessment-fda)).
- Manufacturing scalability and verticalization: screen printing of conductive inks, converting operations (hydrogel/adherents), SMT to non-patient side; roll-to-roll for cost/yield; hermetic packaging for implantables (I-Connect007; Cirtec) ([I-Connect007, 2026](https://iconnect007.com/article/137147/designing-and-manufacturing-wearable-biosensors/137150/flex); [Cirtec Medical, 2026](https://www.cirtecmed.com/solutions/brain-computer-interface-bci)).
- Performance reproducibility: low batch-to-batch variation; durability after bending for wearables (Allumcorp) ([Allumcorp, 2026](https://www.allumcorp.com/products/flexible-screen-printed-disposable-electrodes-flexible-biosensors-wearable-device-electrodes/)).
- Technology roadmap fit: capacity for micron-scale features and high-density interconnects to support next-generation BCI/neuromod devices (Nature BME; Bioengineer.org) ([Nature Biomedical Engineering, 2026](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41551-025-01501-w); [Bioengineer.org, 2025](https://bioengineer.org/revolutionary-65536-electrode-wireless-brain-computer-interface/)).
- Cost structure transparency: tiered pricing; BOM and COO reporting; supply reliability (VentureOutsource) ([VentureOutsource, n.d.-a](https://ventureoutsource.com/contract-manufacturing/helpful-rfq-guidelines-before-negotiating-ems-manufacturer-contract-service-pricing-terms/)).

C23. NPS or customer satisfaction benchmarks

- No public NPS or satisfaction benchmarks specific to flexible medical electrode component suppliers were identified in the compiled sources. OEM procurement uses formal RFP scoring, supplier audits, and performance KPIs more than consumer-style NPS (Ivalua) ([Ivalua, 2026](https://www.ivalua.com/blog/vendor-selection-process)).

C24. Common objections and lost-deal reasons

- Compliance gaps: lack of ISO 13485 certification, weak risk management, or inability to support FDA-inspectable supplier audit documentation under QMSR (FDLI; Hogan Lovells) ([FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/); [Hogan Lovells, 2024](https://www.jdsupra.com/legalnews/new-device-qmsr-includes-significant-5641492/)).
- Biocompatibility uncertainty: insufficient ISO 10993 planning/testing strategy for materials and processes, especially novel flexible substrates (Emergo UL; FDA) ([Emergo by UL, 2025](https://www.emergobyul.com/news/us-fda-updates-final-guidance-iso-10993-medical-device-biocompatibility); [FDA, n.d.-b](https://www.fda.gov/medical-devices/biocompatibility-assessment-resource-center/biocompatibility-evaluation-endpoints-contact-duration-periods)).
- Manufacturing/DFM risk: inability to integrate converting (adhesives/hydrogels) or assure patient comfort (connections on non-patient side), or to meet tight via/feature targets (I-Connect007) ([I-Connect007, 2026](https://iconnect007.com/article/137147/designing-and-manufacturing-wearable-biosensors/137150/flex)).
- Cost and transparency: missing tiered pricing, BOM/COO transparency, or automation and sustainability metrics increasingly demanded in RFQs (VentureOutsource; SourcifyChina trends) ([VentureOutsource, n.d.-a](https://ventureoutsource.com/contract-manufacturing/helpful-rfq-guidelines-before-negotiating-ems-manufacturer-contract-service-pricing-terms/); [SourcifyChina, 2026](https://www.sourcifychina.com/supplier-oem)).
- Channel mismatch: startups may prefer ISO 13485 CDMOs with design-transfer capabilities rather than component-only suppliers; inability to offer CDMO-like support can prolong or derail adoption (Resonetics; Cirtec) ([Resonetics, 2026](https://resonetics.com/services-capabilities/capabilities-products/sensor-technology/wearable-biosensors/); [Cirtec Medical, 2026](https://www.cirtecmed.com/solutions/brain-computer-interface-bci)).

Market context and implications for LG Innotek (LGIT)

Regulatory shifts and category signals

- FDAâ€™s De Novo for autonomous/needle-free CGM and stratified portfolios (wellness-to-medical) indicate expanding markets for wearable biosensors and CGM at multiple regulatory levels (Biolinq; Medical Design & Outsourcing). New categories, like an â€œintegrated primary displayâ€ CGM sensor operating with/without a phone, suggest sensor modules are moving up the stackâ€”yet still reliant on high-quality electrode substrates beneath (Biolinq; Medical Design & Outsourcing) ([Biolinq, 2025](https://www.biolinq.com/news/biolinq-raises-58m-for-glucose-microsensor-pivotal-trial); [Medical Design & Outsourcing, 2025](https://www.medicaldesignandoutsourcing.com/fda-consumer-wearables-guidance-medical-devices/)).

- High-density neural interfaces are accelerating toward clinical relevance. Flexible thin-film microelectrode arrays with micron-scale features are displacing macroelectrodes; subdural-contained, high-channel-count, wireless BCIs concentrate both electrode and electronics on ultra-thin substratesâ€”core to LGITâ€™s CoF/fine-feature strengths (Nature BME; Bioengineer.org) ([Nature Biomedical Engineering, 2026](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41551-025-01501-w); [Bioengineer.org, 2025](https://bioengineer.org/revolutionary-65536-electrode-wireless-brain-computer-interface/)).

- The competitive field includes CDMOs with ISO 13485 credentials and vertical integration (Resonetics; Cirtec), global EMS/ODMs, and medtech incumbents. Procurement norms emphasize BOM transparency, tiered pricing, supplier audits, and supply resilienceâ€”table stakes LGIT must meet to be credible (Resonetics; Cirtec; VentureOutsource; FDLI) ([Resonetics, 2026](https://resonetics.com/services-capabilities/capabilities-products/sensor-technology/wearable-biosensors/); [Cirtec Medical, 2026](https://www.cirtecmed.com/solutions/brain-computer-interface-bci); [VentureOutsource, n.d.-a](https://ventureoutsource.com/contract-manufacturing/helpful-rfq-guidelines-before-negotiating-ems-manufacturer-contract-service-pricing-terms/); [FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/)).

Strategic recommendation: Enter as a component/CDMO hybrid, not a system OEM

Given the regulatory, clinical-evidence, and commercialization barriers in CGM and neuromodulationâ€”and the nascency yet complexity of BCIâ€”LGITâ€™s most defensible, monetizable path is to enter as a specialized, ISO 13485-certified component supplier with CDMO-style co-development services. The thesis:

- Where LGITâ€™s differentiation lands:
  - Fine-feature photolithography (~10 Âµm) and CoF on flexible substrates directly support high-density thin-film ECoG/BCI arrays and compact CGM electrode geometries, which are beyond commodity screen printing and increasingly central to clinical performance and miniaturization (Nature BME; Bioengineer.org; I-Connect007) ([Nature Biomedical Engineering, 2026](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41551-025-01501-w); [Bioengineer.org, 2025](https://bioengineer.org/revolutionary-65536-electrode-wireless-brain-computer-interface/); [I-Connect007, 2026](https://iconnect007.com/article/137147/designing-and-manufacturing-wearable-biosensors/137150/flex)).
  - R2R capabilities align with CGM scale economics if electrode performance can be validated with low batch-to-batch variation; integrating converting operations (adhesives, hydrogels) or partnering with converters will be crucial for patch comfort and yield (I-Connect007; Allumcorp) ([I-Connect007, 2026](https://iconnect007.com/article/137147/designing-and-manufacturing-wearable-biosensors/137150/flex); [Allumcorp, 2026](https://www.allumcorp.com/products/flexible-screen-printed-disposable-electrodes-flexible-biosensors-wearable-device-electrodes/)).

- How to monetize credibly and quickly:
  - Achieve ISO 13485 certification; build a biocompatible material library and a standardized ISO 10993 biological evaluation planning playbook; and prepare for FDA-inspectable supplier audits under QMSRâ€”these unlock DMU blockers (FDLI; Emergo UL; FDA) ([FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/); [Emergo by UL, 2025](https://www.emergobyul.com/news/us-fda-updates-final-guidance-iso-10993-medical-device-biocompatibility); [FDA, n.d.-a](https://www.fda.gov/medical-devices/biocompatibility-assessment-resource-center/basics-biocompatibility-information-needed-assessment-fda)).
  - Offer NRE-friendly co-development kits: electrode design libraries with 10 Âµm-class features; reference stack-ups (PI/adhesives); CoF interposer options; and process DFM notes tailored for wearables (SMT on non-patient side) and implantables (hermetic feedthrough compatibility) (I-Connect007; Cirtec) ([I-Connect007, 2026](https://iconnect007.com/article/137147/designing-and-manufacturing-wearable-biosensors/137150/flex); [Cirtec Medical, 2026](https://www.cirtecmed.com/solutions/brain-computer-interface-bci)).
  - Partner where necessary for verticalization: adhesives/hydrogel converting, hermetic enclosures, and sterilization-compatible packagingâ€”through alliances with established ISO 13485 CDMOs and converters (Resonetics; Cirtec) ([Resonetics, 2026](https://resonetics.com/services-capabilities/capabilities-products/sensor-technology/wearable-biosensors/); [Cirtec Medical, 2026](https://www.cirtecmed.com/solutions/brain-computer-interface-bci)).

- Where to focus first:
  - BCI and neuromodulation pilot programs: lower volumes but high technical barriers; thin-film arrays and high-density interconnects are clear needs, reducing competition with commodity screen-printed suppliers (Nature BME; Cirtec) ([Nature Biomedical Engineering, 2026](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41551-025-01501-w); [Cirtec Medical, 2026](https://www.cirtecmed.com/solutions/brain-computer-interface-bci)).
  - Next-gen CGM substrates: target OEM R&D and startups working on non-traditional form factors (e.g., integrated primary display, modular wearables, AI-native multimodal sensing), where photolithography and R2R can shift size, integration, and yield curves (Biolinq; Trinity Biotech) ([Biolinq, 2025](https://www.biolinq.com/news/biolinq-raises-58m-for-glucose-microsensor-pivotal-trial); [Trinity Biotech, 2025](https://trinitybiotech.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/07/Trinity-Biotech-Unveils-CGM-An-AI-Native-Platform-Targeting-the-260-Billion-AI-Wearable-Market.pdf)).

- How to win in procurement:
  - Deliver RFQ-grade transparency (tiered volume, BOM, COO) and disclose automation metrics (robot density, energy efficiency) now requested in RFQs; prepare for supplier audits to be reviewed by FDA (VentureOutsource; SourcifyChina; FDLI) ([VentureOutsource, n.d.-a](https://ventureoutsource.com/contract-manufacturing/helpful-rfq-guidelines-before-negotiating-ems-manufacturer-contract-service-pricing-terms/); [SourcifyChina, 2026](https://www.sourcifychina.com/supplier-oem); [FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/)).
  - Build a medical-focused sales motion to serve the DMU: engineering champions, QA/regulatory evaluators, and procurement owners, with templated documentation for ISO 10993 plans, process validations, and supplier audit packages (Ivalua; FDA) ([Ivalua, 2026](https://www.ivalua.com/blog/vendor-selection-process); [FDA, n.d.-a](https://www.fda.gov/medical-devices/biocompatibility-assessment-resource-center/basics-biocompatibility-information-needed-assessment-fda)).

Comparative view: Component vs module vs integrated system entry

- Component (electrodes/substrates/interposers): Highest alignment to LGIT core; fastest path to revenue with CDMO-like support; defensible via fine-features/R2R and quality/regulatory posture. Competitive field is fragmented (CDMOs, specialty printers), but medical-grade thin-film density is a premium niche (Cirtec; Resonetics) ([Cirtec Medical, 2026](https://www.cirtecmed.com/solutions/brain-computer-interface-bci); [Resonetics, 2026](https://resonetics.com/services-capabilities/capabilities-products/sensor-technology/wearable-biosensors/)).

- Module (sensor subassembly): In CGM, module-level is increasingly owned by OEMs due to IP in chemistry and algorithms; in BCI, arrays plus interposers may be a viable module offering in collaboration with system integrators. Regulatory liabilities and validation burden rise with module scope (Biolinq; Medical Design & Outsourcing) ([Biolinq, 2025](https://www.biolinq.com/news/biolinq-raises-58m-for-glucose-microsensor-pivotal-trial); [Medical Design & Outsourcing, 2025](https://www.medicaldesignandoutsourcing.com/fda-consumer-wearables-guidance-medical-devices/)).

- Integrated system (CGM/BCI device): Not recommended initially. This would thrust LGIT into clinical evidence generation, payer/reimbursement, and software/algorithm IP competitionâ€”domains where incumbents and focused startups are far ahead, and where FDA Class II/III or De Novo pathways would dramatically extend time-to-market and capital needs (FDLI; CMS IDEs) ([FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/); [CMS, 2026](https://www.cms.gov/medicare/coverage/investigational-device-exemption-ide-studies/approved)).

Operational readiness checklist for LGIT

- Quality/Regulatory
  - ISO 13485 certification; FDA registration and QMSR alignment; documented risk management per ISO 14971; supplier audit packages prepared for potential FDA inspection (FDLI; Hogan Lovells) ([FDLI, 2025](https://www.fdli.org/2025/12/the-qmsr-transition-new-fda-guidance-and-their-impacts-on-active-implantable-medical-devices/); [Hogan Lovells, 2024](https://www.jdsupra.com/legalnews/new-device-qmsr-includes-significant-5641492/)).
  - ISO 10993 biocompatibility strategy with a materials/process library and testing partners; ASCA participation awareness (FDA/Emergo) ([Emergo by UL, 2025](https://www.emergobyul.com/news/us-fda-updates-final-guidance-iso-10993-medical-device-biocompatibility); [FDA, n.d.-a](https://www.fda.gov/medical-devices/biocompatibility-assessment-resource-center/basics-biocompatibility-information-needed-assessment-fda)).

- Manufacturing/DFM
  - Validate 10 Âµm-class features on PI with medical-grade adhesives; demonstrate roll-to-roll stability and <5% batch variability targets for wearables; provide via strategies and SMT layouts for non-patient side (Allumcorp; I-Connect007) ([Allumcorp, 2026](https://www.allumcorp.com/products/flexible-screen-printed-disposable-electrodes-flexible-biosensors-wearable-device-electrodes/); [I-Connect007, 2026](https://iconnect007.com/article/137147/designing-and-manufacturing-wearable-biosensors/137150/flex)).
  - Establish or partner for converting (hydrogels, adhesives), hermetic interconnects/feedthroughs for implantables, and sterilization-friendly packaging (Cirtec; Resonetics) ([Cirtec Medical, 2026](https://www.cirtecmed.com/solutions/brain-computer-interface-bci); [Resonetics, 2026](https://resonetics.com/services-capabilities/capabilities-products/sensor-technology/wearable-biosensors/)).

- Commercial
  - Build a medical-focused RFP/RFQ engine: tiered pricing, BOM/COO disclosure, automation and energy metrics; align with Procurementâ€™s push for payment term optimization and risk frameworks (VentureOutsource; ProcureDesk; SourcifyChina; ProjectManagerTemplate) ([VentureOutsource, n.d.-a](https://ventureoutsource.com/contract-manufacturing/helpful-rfq-guidelines-before-negotiating-ems-manufacturer-contract-service-pricing-terms/); [ProcureDesk, 2026](https://www.procuredesk.com/spend-analysis/); [SourcifyChina, 2026](https://www.sourcifychina.com/supplier-oem); [ProjectManagerTemplate, 2026](https://www.projectmanagertemplate.com/post/oem-procurement-consulting-enhancing-supplier-relationship-management)).
  - Target buyers: OEM R&D directors and system integrators in BCI/neuromod; CGM platform R&D for next-gen patches/modules; startups bridging wellness-medical (e.g., multimodal CGM+) where substrate miniaturization + manufacturability is a wedge (Trinity Biotech; Medical Design & Outsourcing) ([Trinity Biotech, 2025](https://trinitybiotech.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/07/Trinity-Biotech-Unveils-CGM-An-AI-Native-Platform-Targeting-the-260-Billion-AI-Wearable-Market.pdf); [Medical Design & Outsourcing, 2025](https://www.medicaldesignandoutsourcing.com/fda-consumer-wearables-guidance-medical-devices/)).

Conclusion

LGITâ€™s core competenciesâ€”10 Âµm-class photolithography, CoF on flexible substrates, and R2R productionâ€”map directly to the most demanding and fastest-growing needs in flexible electrodes for medical devices: high-density thin-film arrays for BCI/neuromodulation and miniaturized, reliable CGM sensor substrates. The buying process is long and multidisciplinary; regulatory expectations (QMSR, ISO 13485/10993) raise the bar on supplier auditability and material safety; and procurement demands transparency and scalability. Entering as a specialized, ISO 13485-certified component supplier with CDMO-style co-developmentâ€”rather than attempting integrated systemsâ€”offers the best balance of defensibility, time-to-revenue, and strategic leverage, with partnerships to complete vertical capabilities where needed. By aligning early with R&D champions, satisfying QA/RA auditors, and equipping Procurement with transparent RFQ responses, LGIT can turn substrate-level strengths into clinical-grade differentiation and durable supplier positions.
